I usually just think I finished everything and move on to the next thing, so the worry isn't really there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, even outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't feel sad or down, and I still like doing all the stuff I normally do when I'm not at the plant. My mood is totally fine, honestly. It's really just the work stuff that's the problem.\n\nDoctor: When you leave those final steps unfinished at work, do you have thoughts that something bad might happen, or do you feel like you have to leave them unfinished in a certain way?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I don't have any thoughts about bad things happening, and I definitely don't feel like I have to leave things unfinished. It's not on purpose at all. Like I was saying, I just honestly think I've finished the task, so I walk away. There's no specific routine to it or anything.\n\nDoctor: Has this kind of thing ever happened in other jobs or situations, or is this something that's only started happening at your current job?\n\nPatient: It's been happening for a while now. This has been a serious problem since I switched into full-time meat processing, which was about two years and four months ago. So, yeah, it's not just a recent thing.\n\nDoctor: Before you started working in meat processing, did you ever have any problems finishing tasks at other jobs or in school?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely happened in school, too. Um, my mom actually still has my old elementary school report cards, from around fourth grade, I think. They always said that I was bright, but that I needed constant reminders to finish my classwork and actually turn in my homework. I remember understanding the lessons perfectly fine, but I would just... miss chunks of the instructions unless the teacher stayed right close to me. So, yeah, it's been an issue for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you ever find yourself losing track of conversations, forgetting things easily, or feeling restless, especially in situations where you need to stay seated or focused for a while?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed that, to be honest. I don't feel restless when I have to sit still or anything like that. And I don't think I lose track of conversations... at least, nobody has ever pointed it out to me. So, um, no, I don't really have those specific issues outside of work, or if I do, I just haven't noticed them.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to pay attention or get easily distracted during activities that require sustained mental effort, like reading, watching a movie, or having a conversation?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely with things like reading. Um, for example, at work, I always put off the certification modules and any of the long procedure reading until the absolute last possible minute. It's just... sitting there, trying to actually focus and absorb all those rules, it honestly feels almost physically exhausting to me. So I definitely avoid that kind of mental effort for as long as I can.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work and reading tasks, do you ever have trouble focusing or staying on track during other activities that require attention, like conversations, planning events, or managing your schedule?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. Like I said a minute ago, conversations are fine for me. And with planning things or, you know, managing my schedule... I don't really have a problem keeping track of my day-to-day stuff outside of work. I just kind of handle it. So, yeah, I haven't noticed any trouble with those kinds of things. It's really just the heavy reading and the work tasks that get me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
